## Confirmed decisions and current work

- Treat `anim@move_m@prisoner_cuffed` and all other documented clips as candidates until the game confirms the dictionary and clip; never force this clip onto an attached Citizen because the Gameplay Flow records a floating result. Choose a natural GTA task or another runtime-confirmed option when the documented clip does not look right.
- **Confirmed alley scene animation choice:** add both meeting and exchange behavior. Use the Gameplay Branch listed mp_ped_interaction handshake_guy_a / handshake_guy_b pair for alley_criminal_meeting; use mp_common givetake1_a / givetake1_b for the drug and money/weapons exchange events. Apply them through AmbientWorld as dictionary clips, not scenario animations. This is the user-approved use of those authored clips in criminal scenes; runtime appearance still requires GTA verification.
- **Confirmed transport decisions:** after requesting station transport, offer personal-Utility acceptance or van request only when the saved Preferred Utility is valid, available, close enough and has enough actual free custody seats. Declining personal transport keeps custody and requests the van. Unsupported, missing, invalid, unusable, out-of-range or under-capacity Utility arrangements automatically use the van. Do not mix transport decline/cancellation with Ped release. The code uses the existing 35 m player escort range and checks real rear seat availability.
- **Confirmed Dispatch release decisions:** expose Dispatch own `Release The Ped` action under `Dispatch Activity` / `Your Dispatch Authority Response`, separately from Citizen release. Release only the currently active secured Dispatch suspect; if it is inside the Player Police Utility, wait for a safe physical exit. Do not free other incident suspects. Solo release ends Dispatch after its response; with remaining custody, keep Dispatch active through handoff. Animation use must pass live dictionary/clip checks and must not produce floating or unnatural movement.
- **Confirmed Station/Profile decision:** changing the saved Preferred personal Profile in the Main UI while away from the Station leaves the Player at their current location. The first Station introduction remains a separate doorway-trigger flow; the user requested a marked ground cylinder, a close-up Wardrobe view, and a Garage view that switches the displayed Police vehicle while browsing.
- **Confirmed station-department rule:** assign Beaver Bush Ranger Station, LSIA Field Office, and Bolingbroke Penitentiary to LSPD. Keep all three as separate station records with their supplied names and positions; the new XML records follow this choice.
- **Confirmed station custody rule:** Dispatch uses the selected station’s outside Location as its handoff area. Find safe flat ground nearby, show the Player a visible marker, and require the transport to stop there before unloading. Standby officers physically receive the suspect. Do not use the Player Garage or a road; keep the separate standalone Convoy Activity unchanged for its later phase.
- **Confirmed Profile rule:** keep separate saved Backup choices for Citizen Interaction and Dispatch. The new preference affects Dispatch and Citizen Interaction only; Crime Activity keeps reading the old `backupPedId` value for now. Older profile XML without the new fields loads that old value into both choices.
- **Confirmed station selection behavior:** a saved station change also changes the saved department; Dispatch call locations remain near the Player even when far from that station. Custody still routes to the currently selected station, including a longer trip. The Player must choose a new station to change the destination. In GTA, verify each station’s outside Location has usable nearby ground before treating its point as runtime-confirmed.
- **Confirmed Station change flow:** choose a different Station in the Main UI, travel there, and use the introduction marker at that Station’s threshold to open its Wardrobe/Garage preview. The Downtown preview room is a visual selection scene only; the real Police vehicle appears outside the selected Station after setup, and custody/Convoy marks remain separate.
- **Confirmed Convoy loading timeout decision:** give each suspect their own saved physical loading wait. The Player's 20-second offer is outside that wait. If one escort is physically handling another suspect, the waiting suspect's clock starts when the escort becomes free. This can allow 20 seconds for the offer plus up to 45 seconds per suspect when the saved timeout is 45 seconds. Keep the existing longer allowance for a suspect physically escorted by the Player.
- **Confirmed release-audio decision:** if one suspect is released while other suspects remain, show the Dispatcher response as on-screen text and keep speech quiet because the current catalog has no matching Dispatcher line. A solo release uses the existing Dispatch-closed audio event.
- **Audio asset note:** Player speech for personal transport request, active travel and station arrival is not present in the current `ResponseAudio.xml`/WAV catalog. The user will provide or identify matching recordings. Leave the authored catalog and recordings untouched until supplied or identified. Utility-unavailable/van-arriving wording also needs an existing Dispatcher cue or an approved catalog match; an on-screen message can communicate it.
- Preserve all Crime Activity code, XML, registrations and integrations. Preserve unrelated gameplay and authored data.
- Milestone 1 code changed: `LSImmersivePoliceCore.TogglePatrol` no longer creates the opening call; `LSPDDispatch.Process` schedules the first automatic offer after the confirmed 30 seconds; `RequestOfferNow` still serves the explicit UI path. Acceptance: no automatic offer on Patrol activation, eligibility after 30 seconds, explicit request remains immediate. Isolated x64 build passes; focused timing validation remains pending.
- Milestone 2 code changed: scoped numeric settings in `LSMainSetting` use bounded GTA text input, update configuration owners, refresh displayed values, and retain the existing Save Settings path. Crime Activity and unrelated environment/performance sliders were not changed. Isolated x64 build passes; value and persistence checks remain pending.
- Milestone 3 build repair: added existing `LSWorldAmbientBehavior.cs` to the explicit project compile list. The isolated build compiled successfully without writing to the configured GTA installation.
- Convoy stage audit (dated runtime evidence; not validation of the current source/build):
  - Arrest/handcuff: 2026-10-02 log records physical `TASK_ARREST_PED` handcuff starts for both group suspects, the first secured, the second completion, and `Prisoners=2`.
  - Dispatch to Convoy ownership: the same 2026-10-02 log records both Ped handles in `POLICE_CUSTODY_HANDOFF` and Convoy custody start.
  - Escort/load: 2026-09-23 group log records contact, physical escort/animation and rear-door progression for one suspect, then physical entry for only one of two. The phase timed out at 45 seconds and returned both living suspects to Dispatch. Current source now starts a separate timeout for each active physical loading attempt, excludes the Player offer, pauses the queued suspect while another suspect's escort is busy, and clears that suspect's timer after confirmed vehicle entry. The source validator and isolated x64 build pass; this only validates source/build, not current GTA behavior.
  - Single-person station path: 2026-09-21 log records physical entry, then the Player's physical exit at `14:33:36.897`; station completion is logged six milliseconds later with no receiving-officer escort-start event in between. This is evidence of premature station completion, not proof of officer receipt. A 2026-09-22 group log similarly confirms the group immediately after the second physical exit, before a matching escort-start event for that second suspect. Current code now waits for each receiving escort to start and for each suspect to reach the existing station handoff point.
  - Prison handoff: a 2026-09-18 historical log records physical vehicle exit, receiving-officer escort start, prison handoff confirmation about 17 seconds later, and Dispatch completion. - Documented behavior: `project_sources\LSImmersiveLife Visual Design Documentation.txt`, “Dispatch Activity Response” lines 271–304 and “Back Up Response” lines 306–348; `project_sources\LSImmersiveLife Project Mapping.txt`, Dispatch and Back Up ownership lines 593–635; `project_sources\LSImmersiveLIfe Gameplay Flow.txt`, Patrol callout timing and active response lines 24–30 and 63–75; `project_sources\LSImmersiveLIfe Gameplay Branch.txt`, Patrol-to-Dispatch and contextual Back Up branches lines 291–323 and 408–440; `project_sources\LSImmersiveLife Gameplay Audio Branch.txt`, Dispatcher, Player decision, and Backup response lines 31–40, 88–104, and 221–239. Citizen response prompts now describe B as opening Back Up Response.

## Police Profile favorite-list hierarchy (2026-10-06)

- Documented behavior: `project_sources\LSImmersiveLife Visual Design Documentation.txt`, “Saved Favourite Set” lines 411–435 and “Police Immersive Profile” lines 540–575; `project_sources\LSImmersiveLife Project Mapping.txt`, “LSPDProfile.cs / Profile Selection During On Duty and Patrol Duty” and “Police Profile Framework UI” lines 426–457; `project_sources\LSImmersiveLIfe Gameplay Branch.txt`, `NewPlayerModeOn` and `DailyPlayerMode` lines 268–288; `project_sources\LSImmersiveLIfe Gameplay Flow.txt`, Police Mode introduction lines 1–21. The Profile holds preferred resources and reusable favorites; the Station interface owns the first-time System Police Ped and vehicle catalog selections.
- Confirmed Owner hierarchy: the main **Police Immersive Profile** screen shows the preferred Character Model and Vehicle Model, keeps Weapon Loadout and the two documented Back Up Ped choices, and keeps Personal Loadout Weapon and Save the Profile Data. **Saved Favorite Lists** contains **DLC Characters**, **DLC Vehicles**, and **DLC Inventory Weapons**. Selecting a saved Character or Vehicle updates the preferred Profile through the existing save/apply path. Selecting a saved inventory weapon selects it through the existing personal weapon path. Character and Vehicle favorites can be saved from the current selection; full model catalogs remain in Station setup.
- Data boundary: the existing Profile XML stores favorites with a `source` value and the personal weapon collection, but has no separate DLC-only flag. The three requested category names therefore group the existing saved entries; the UI shows each entry's source instead of filtering or relabeling saved assets. `LSPDImmersiveProfile.xml` and `LSPolicePersonalWeapons.xml` were not changed.

- Changes: removed the full Ped and Vehicle catalogs from the Police Profile menus; added live preferred Character and Vehicle rows and the three-level Saved Favorite Lists hierarchy. The Weapon Loadout menu now shows the preferred weapon and typing action without listing the entire library; the personal weapon collection remains editable through Personal Loadout. Favorite entries show their model or weapon identifier, type, source, and preferred state. Updated `Tests\ValidatePoliceSource.ps1` to enforce the hierarchy, entry details, and Station/Profile catalog boundary. ## Citizen database record and Citizen Ped Response framework (2026-10-06)

- Documented behavior: `project_sources\LSImmersiveLife Visual Design Documentation.txt`, “Ambient Citizen Response” lines 376–398 and “NPCDatabase” lines 578–615; `project_sources\LSImmersiveLife Project Mapping.txt`, “Citizen Ped Interaction” lines 1152–1205 and “NPC Citizen Record / Traffic Record” lines 1320–1382; `project_sources\LSImmersiveLIfe Gameplay Branch.txt`, `NPCFootInteraction` lines 1–15 and `NPCVehicleRoadInteraction` lines 99–131; `project_sources\LSImmersiveLIfe Gameplay Flow.txt`, “NPC Interaction - Citizen Ped Interaction” lines 109–118; `project_sources\LSImmersiveLife Gameplay Audio Branch.txt`, “CIVILIAN / NPC INTERACTION” lines 361–389. The visual reference is `project_sources\LSImmersiveLife Police Citizen Interaction.png`. It calls for the selected Citizen’s portrait, identity (including gender/age, occupation and area), document and legal status, Police response, live contact/document state, and—only for a traffic contact—the selected driver’s actual plate, vehicle model and color.
- User request: make this live record smaller than the Police Almanac but comfortable to read; open **Citizen Ped Response** with configured G; make all documented framework choices selectable and connected to Citizen owner actions; show the selected Citizen’s database record only after Dispatcher validation.
- Verified ownership and sequence: `LSNPCDatabase` reads XML and returns the stable session record; `LSPDNPCResponse` owns the selected Citizen, document handoff, validation and physical response; `LSImmersivePoliceCore` gates/routes commands; `LSImmersivePoliceUI` owns display and menu input. The documented order is greet → request ID/document → wait for handoff → request Dispatcher validation → view the record → accept/reject. E remains the quick contact/arrest control in its documented stages; G opens the framework. Requesting validation is not a Dispatch incident.
- Changes: G now queues the **Citizen Ped Response** framework and waits for key release before opening it. The documented menu rows now call the existing Citizen owner instead of permanent disabled placeholders. The live lower-left record follows the supplied panel structure, uses screen-scaled layout/text, the active Ped’s GTA headshot, a Clear/Review/Detention badge, XML-backed gender and age range, citizen identity, document condition, status, warrant and recommendation, and live contact/document state. Traffic display uses the selected contact’s plate, model and primary color. Accept/Reject wait until the Dispatcher record is displayed, while the documented separate Backup custody offer remains available. No animation, Dispatch, Convoy, Backup physical custody, or Crime Activity implementation was changed for this milestone; no image plugin or additional art file was needed.
- Checks: `Tests\ValidatePoliceSource.ps1` passed after the final source changes: 31 C# files, 0 semantic errors, and all XML/source/menu/owner checks passed. ## Injury native compile fix and custody safeguards (2026-10-07)

- Owner-directed requirement: injury handling must preserve the no-critical-hits state for Dispatch and Citizen subjects so limb hits can remain nonfatal and arrestable, while the requested fatal-hit behavior remains distinct. This requirement comes from the Owner's gameplay request; it is not treated as confirmed by a reference animation or by compilation.
- Code owners/call paths: `LSPDDispatch.cs` tracks and restores injury config for Dispatch suspects through incident maintenance; `LSPDNPCResponse.cs` does the same for Citizen subjects through response maintenance. Both paths use the available `GET_PED_CONFIG_FLAG` / `SET_PED_CONFIG_FLAG` native with flag 2 (`NoCriticalHits`) and retain each subject's original flag value for restoration.
- Compiler report: Visual Studio had reported missing `Hash.GET_PED_SUFFERS_CRITICAL_HITS` at the two injury handlers. That unsupported enum was removed from both current source files. A source sweep found no remaining `GET_PED_SUFFERS_CRITICAL_HITS` reference.
- Custody safeguards in the same milestone: Dispatch, Citizen player arrest, and Backup now wait for GTA's physical cuff state before marking a subject secured or transferring to Backup escort; retries are bounded. Dead or zero-health subjects are excluded from live/compliance handling, and writhe tasks are not issued to subjects still in vehicles. Spawned Backup and ordinary Police Response officers are explicitly assigned the COP relationship group so the existing Police ally relationship protection can apply.
- Acceptance scenario: in GTA V Enhanced, confirm limb-injured Dispatch and Citizen subjects remain alive, on the ground, and arrestable; confirm the requested chest/stomach/head fatality behavior; then confirm player/Backup arrest visibly cuffs before escort and that Police response units do not attack the Player or their police vehicle. These runtime outcomes remain **pending owner observation**; the source/build does not prove their visual or gameplay result.
- Checks/deployment: Visual Studio 2022 MSBuild Release x64 succeeded with the location-copy post-build action redirected to `C:\NoGame`. Tests were not run. The built DLL SHA256 is `9E6EF2315D15BE08571884259E8AF4BFE04F8CE7D90DAB17D6904A8631622122` (1,083,392 bytes). Only the confirmed runtime `scripts\LSImmersiveLife.dll` was deployed, with matching SHA256; the replaced DLL is preserved at `obj\TaskBackup-20261007-InjuryCustodyAllies\LSImmersiveLife.before-deploy.dll`. GTA was closed during deployment. In-game validation remains pending.
## Physical interior access for Player and role actors (2026-10-07)

- Owner-confirmed mapping rule: use paired zero-interior `ObservedCoordinateCandidate` and nonzero-interior `InteriorPointCandidate` captures linked by `NearbyDifferentInteriorContext`, with the Owner-confirmed approximately 1–2 m gap, as the outside-to-threshold route. This is the Owner’s mapping interpretation; the stored `NeedsReview` text is not proof of a GTA door or traversable route. The Owner has confirmed the interiors in-game; do not ask them to re-confirm sites or request point IDs.
- Official references: `project_sources\LSImmersiveLife Project Mapping.txt`, “Interior access, thresholds, and restrictions” (line 778) and the interior/foot-activity rules around lines 1090–1106; `project_sources\LSImmersiveLIfe Gameplay Flow.txt`, Store Robbery entry (lines 30–40); `project_sources\LSImmersiveLIfe Gameplay Branch.txt`, Store Robbery Group Suspect Shot Fired (lines 628–677). The Mapping assigns AmbientWorld access/placement assessment, PoliceCore coordination, and actor movement to existing gameplay owners. Store Robbery requires a real interior event site; broad area anchors alone do not identify a store.
- Data result: `LSImmersiveLocation.xml` currently yields one unique pair within the confirmed 1–2 m rule: outside `survey.site.20261002.000072` / `LC-20261002-000309` to inside `survey.site.20261002.000310` / `LC-20261002-000318`, observed interior `155649`, 2.021 m apart, West Mirror Drive. Other nearby cross-interior records are farther away or repeat captures around that site; they were not promoted to entrance links. Site physical type and event association remain `Unknown` in the XML.
- Current implementation: `LSImmersiveLocationCatalog.BuildInteriorAccessPairs` resolves only the linked capture pair and keeps the route as a runtime candidate. `LSWorldAmbientBehavior` checks both live interior IDs, raycasts the threshold, requires GTA’s registered unlocked door, and now advances its open ratio in short steps while holding the door for physical traversal. It confirms the actor reached the other interior context and destination area before restoring the door. It never teleports or issues an actor movement task. `LSImmersivePoliceCore.Process` routes contextual E to the Player path while Police Authority is active; the Player must walk through. Code owners are `LSWorldAmbientBehavior.cs` (location/door service), `LSImmersivePoliceCore.cs` (Player input coordination), and `LSImmersiveLocationCatalog.cs` (pair parsing).
- Owner-specific actor audit: `LSPDNPCResponse` has no interior destination in Citizen contact or custody; `LSPDDispatch` currently resolves outdoor scene positions and creates its own scene actors, while the supplied 06:58:16 bank robbery log reports no matching typed site; `LSPDBackUp` targets the active street/subject and its vehicle; `LSPDConvoy` Escort and standby continue toward the documented transport/station handoff. ## Roleplay doorway shell-ID and adjacent-capture correction (2026-10-07)

- Owner-observed acceptance failure: screenshots 839–843 show that an addon preferred Ped and Franklin could use a trainer-opened door outside Police Roleplay, but Police/Roleplay mode did not reliably open the same barber or discount-store door. Its physically useful exterior building anchor is in adjacent capture `survey.site.20261002.000147`, about 1.38 m from the tested Player position. Pairing each interior candidate to nearby `BuildingCandidateEvidence` on the same authored street and zone, within 8 m, covers 79 of the 91 interior candidates. The Player must still physically walk through; no teleport or coordinate-only completion was introduced. Door state changes remain bounded and are restored after the session.

The checked Gameplay Flow/Branch documents and current event XML define no separate required witness role or witness pool, so this pass preserves the authored victim/participant data instead of inventing one. Existing criminal profile, Dispatch event, and location catalogs remain the data owners; no actors, cars, or locations were hardcoded, and Convoy was not changed.

Production Logs IV records the repeated synchronous NPC_TRAFFIC_COLLISION_GUARD concern at line 4435 and earlier source-level claims at 5291–5307; those claims did not prove visual gameplay. Mapping: Authority responsibility (paras 862–912), LSPDNPCResponse owner (1144–1158), protected scene actors/cleanup (1237–1244). Gameplay Flow: active Police/Dispatch and custody continuation (paras 64–90). Gameplay Branch: //DispatchEventActivity (line 291), active group/shots-fired alternatives (305–324), vehicle/foot response alternatives (363, 408). These sections set ownership and continuation boundaries; no Citizen contact branch changed.
- **Baseline evidence and first unexpected transitions:** Session 20261012-140622165-e8693f83 recorded NPC_ACTIVE_SCENE_CIVILIAN_MOVED_AWAY for Ped 126724 at 2026-10-12 22:08:43.764 without trigger/task detail, and emergency guards at distances 0.0–2.5 m (for example Vehicle 374020 at 22:08:44.128). The same log does not prove an actual collision or identify the aim/fire cause. Session 20261012-224745989-a42072f8 recorded Vehicle 730882 at 2.9 m on 2026-10-13 06:54:04.492 immediately before Dispatch cancellation at 06:54:05.040; that guard entry also lacks driver/target/task identity and is not proof of impact. Current source confirmed the actionable defects: the aim path could randomly flee; Police de-escalation waited for combat; the emergency traffic guard acted only inside 3.25 m; operation traffic discovery had been centered on Player; and enableCivilianReactionControl had no consumer. No existing ambient-status entry described these as visually verified.
- **Call path and owners:** LSImmersivePoliceUI.Process -> LSImmersivePoliceCore.Process -> LSPDNPCResponse.Process. PoliceCore supplies the active Authority flag, active-road-scene state, and read-only protection lists. LSPDAuthority owns the setting. LSPDNPCResponse owns ambient reactions and local traffic interventions. LSPDPoliceResponse, LSPDBackUp, LSPDConvoy, and Dispatch keep their actors, vehicles, tasks, and progression; their protected references are not reassigned by NPCResponse.
- **Correction:** enableCivilianReactionControl is now consumed by PoliceCore and gates ambient civilian reactions; fresh defaults are enabled in LSPDAuthority and LSImmersiveMainUI.xml. A previously saved explicit false remains false and must be enabled in the user’s existing settings. LSPDNPCResponse gives at most two ordinary pedestrians per 1-second scan a brief nearby-presence notice (6 m); aim without firing produces a calm 1.5-second look/hesitation, not a flee roll; a pedestrian approaching within 2.15 m steps 2.5 m away over a bounded 2.2-second task. Actual nearby gunfire retreats ordinary civilians from the nearest shooting ped for 7 seconds. Gang threats and protected Dispatch/custody/Police actors remain with their owners.
- **Traffic correction:** PoliceCore refreshes up to four operation anchors every 500 ms from Convoy, Backup, PoliceResponse, and the active Dispatch occurrence. NPCResponse rotates one operation anchor per traffic sample and checks it separately from Player space; a remote unit no longer expands the Player-centered radius. Each 500 ms sample checks a local Player sample and at most one operation sample, with a configured maximum of three vehicle interventions (quiet Player patrol remains capped at one). Approaching-risk prediction is limited to 3 seconds, compares closing paths and vehicle-size clearance, rejects separate road levels, and caches up to 64 model-size results. Eligible approaching drivers receive the existing 900 ms temporary brake action with a per-vehicle 3.5-second cooldown. Bikes and larger vehicles use measured model bounds when available. The 3.25 m emergency speed/no-collision guard mechanics remain unchanged; its logs now include driver, vehicle/model, target, distance, speed, task, and duration. The existing active-road-scene departure task remains limited to its prior activeRoadScene path and is not enabled for remote operation samples.
- **Reset and cost bounds:** Authority exit calls NpcResponse.Reset; local operation anchors, brake cooldowns, reaction cooldowns, and collision-guard state are cleared. Player and operation traffic samples run no faster than every 500 ms; civilian presence runs no faster than every 1,000 ms; de-escalation remains on its 1,250 ms gate. The largest civilian task batch is two; traffic intervention count remains configuration-capped. Model-dimension native calls are cached, failures use conservative fallback bounds, and logs are transition/throttle based. No thread, all-world scan, repeated XML read, persistent new GTA handle, or Crime Activity edit was added.
- **Focused checks/build:** 14/14 source/config assertions passed, including setting-to-runtime wiring, calm aim branch, finite gunfire retreat, Gang/scene exclusions, owner protection, bounded context refresh, road-level prediction, guard behavior, reset, and XML parsing. ## Ambient Police recognition of Dispatch custody — 2026-10-13 10:08 SGT

- **Requirement and official sections:** Nearby ambient Police may support an active Dispatch threat, but must recognize each surrendered, physically secured, escorted, and transported Dispatch suspect as out of active combat. The Player remains the incident lead; only Dispatch/Backup/Convoy may advance their respective arrest and custody steps. Visual Design: `//Dispatch Activity Response` and `//Back Up Response` (paras 269–331). Production Logs IV (4866–5008) describe the existing split between Authority and nearby Police Response.
- **Baseline and latest runtime evidence:** `LSPDAuthority.ReconcilePoliceProtection` already protected the Player and set a Player↔COP Respect relationship, but `EnsurePoliceAllyRelationship` skipped live-value checks when the saved groups were unchanged. `ReconcileWantedProtection` already re-applied `SET_MAX_WANTED_LEVEL(0)` if the live cap returned. `LSPDPoliceResponse.MaintainAuthoritySupport` received only Convoy and requested Backup protected actors; it recognized officers fighting the Player, not officers targeting an individual compliant or arrested Dispatch suspect, and its 3-second cooldown allowed repeated task clears. PoliceCore did not pass the live Dispatch occurrence to that method.
- Latest logs are `LSRuntime.log` (last write 09:32:58) and `LSDebug.log` (09:31:38), session `20261013-013033392-7718b624`. At 09:31:31.600 and 09:32:02.324/09:32:32.545, the log reports `POLICE_AUTHORITY_WANTED_CAP`; it does not report `POLICE_AUTHORITY_WANTED_CLEARED`, a wanted-level value, accepted Dispatch, or custody transition. The installed DLL is SHA-256 `AB85317783605A2F5ABC09E2095257C39C2A7F5A63CF685E9AD1B4CA4158D155`, timestamp 09:26:58; the runtime logs do not record a loaded-module hash.
- **Correction and owners:** PoliceCore now passes `Dispatch.Current` to the existing `LSPDPoliceResponse` authority-support method. For the active occurrence, Police Response protects only the per-suspect `CompliantSuspects`, `ArrestedSuspects`, Player handcuff target, and Backup physical-arrest target. Explicit `resist`, `flee`, `fight`, `shoot`, or `combat` state releases that suspect from this protection so Police can engage a renewed threat; other group suspects stay independently eligible. A temporary `LSIL_DISPATCH_CUSTODY` relationship is assigned only to those individuals, with reversible relationship snapshots for the Player, COP, and observed nearby Police groups. A nearby officer task is cleared once when it is actually targeting the Player or one of those protected suspects; target/occurrence memory prevents the prior periodic clear loop. Dispatch actors/participants are excluded from officer recognition. Actor groups and temporary relationship pairs restore when the suspect leaves the protected state, no custody actor remains, support is disabled, or Police Authority resets. No task, model, weapon, actor ownership, Back Up assignment, Dispatch progression, Convoy, Citizen Interaction, or Crime Activity logic was moved or replaced. `EnsurePoliceAllyRelationship` now reads live Player↔COP values, reapplies Respect only when changed, and saves the latest observed values for Authority shutdown restoration.
- **Runtime cost and boundaries:** The existing nearby-ped scan remains configuration-throttled (default 1,250 ms). The new per-core-tick signature reads only the small current Dispatch suspect/status collections; it performs no world scan or native call and makes the existing local scan run promptly after a custody-state transition. Relationship reconciliation is limited to secured suspects and unique nearby Police relationship groups. No extra controller, thread, all-world scan, repeated XML read, broad invincibility, or Crime Activity change was added.

## Ambient traffic yielding around Convoy escort and transport — 2026-10-13 10:38 SGT

- **Requirement and evidence:** The user reports ambient vehicles striking scene officers and the transport van during Convoy escort/loading. Screenshot `codex-clipboard-8d2e0b17-2a3f-4c2a-92bf-6812fcb3aeaf.png` shows the congested custody scene at about 10:25. Matching `LSRuntime.log` session `20261013-021904976-f781d9cd` records the first insufficient response at 10:25:10.512: driver `211206`, vehicle `358406`, target escort ped `504838`, distance `4.7 m`, speed `2.0 m/s`, predicted miss `2.0 m`, required clearance `5.3 m`; another brake-only approach reached Backup/scene ped `87558` at `4.3 m` and `3.0 m/s` at 10:25:14.592. At 10:25:22.264 traffic vehicle `419078` was predicted to pass within `5.9 m` of transport van `231173` against `7.8 m` required clearance. The user-observed impacts are not emitted as a collision event in this log, so the log proves the unsafe approach and underpowered response, not the physical impact itself.
- **Owner and failing gate:** PoliceCore already refreshes Convoy/Backup/PoliceResponse actors and vehicles into NPC Response every 500 ms. Mapping sections `LSPDNPCResponse.cs`, protected actors/vehicles and cleanup, and `LSPDConvoy.cs` keep NPC Response as the traffic owner and Convoy as the owner of custody actors/tasks. The failing path was `LSPDNPCResponse.ApplyTrafficSample` -> `ApplyTrafficCollisionGuard`: a conflict received only a 900 ms temporary brake with a 3,500 ms per-vehicle cooldown; the pair-specific no-collision guard was not applied until the target was within 3.25 m. The 4.3–8.7 m predicted-conflict records therefore fell through without the stronger protection.
- **Correction:** The same owner now uses a 1,200 ms brake with a 1,500 ms cooldown while a predicted conflict remains. For a validated conflict within 1.5 seconds, it caps only that ordinary traffic vehicle to 2.0 m/s and activates the existing short no-collision pair for the exact protected Convoy/Police target. The existing 500 ms local samples, three-vehicle intervention cap, finite guard lifetime, protected entity lists, normal driving routes, and reset cleanup remain. No Convoy, Backup, Dispatch, Citizen, or Crime Activity code changed; there is no city-wide scan, route reassignment, permanent speed limit, blanket collision disable, new entity state, or new logging stream.
- **Documents and call path:** Mapping — `LSPDNPCResponse.cs`, protected actors/vehicles and cleanup, and `LSPDConvoy.cs` custody ownership; Gameplay Flow — Convoy physical escort/loading and Station/Prison continuation; Gameplay Branch — `//DispatchEventActivity` transport options and `//ConvoyActivity`; Visual Design — `Convoy Activity Response`. Runtime path: `LSImmersivePoliceCore.Process` supplies Convoy’s current protected entities -> `LSPDNPCResponse.Process` -> `ApplyLocalTrafficCaution` -> `ApplyTrafficSample` -> `ApplyTrafficCollisionGuard`.
- **Runtime cost and checks:** No added scan or frame loop was introduced. Existing 500 ms local traffic sampling reuses its already-calculated time-to-impact and clearance values. Eight focused source assertions passed for the sampling interval, intervention cap, renewed brake timing, predicted-impact threshold, target-specific guard, finite guard lifetime, and absence of forced route changes or driver task clears.
